- Redaction form: `xxxx...[len N]#hhhhhhhh` = first 4 characters, the value's length, the first 8 hex of sha256(value) (the routing's form -- first 4 + length + sha256[:8] -- with the punctuation the sibling raws and both checkers quote). SSH-port-class values (every 4-5 digit number on an ssh/port/alias line, whatever its verdict) use `lenN:hhhhhhhh` -- the length and the first 8 hex of sha256 only, no digit of the value. No candidate secret, RunPod id or volume name (nor the name's stem), IP address, SSH port, SSH key fingerprint, laptop/container hostname or personal email appears verbatim in this file or its JSON (asserted by the script before writing). A value of 4 characters or fewer is printed in full by the generic form; only non-sensitive values reach it (listed in the round-2 values-check).
- Caveat (enumeration; round 3, computed per sensitive value): the hash in the redacted form is a 32-bit prefix, so a value whose hidden characters have few possible values can be recovered by brute force (expected chance matches = candidates / 2^32), and a value built from words by a word-list search, whatever its length. Recoverable by brute force: runpod_volume_id (1 value(s); 6 hidden of an alphabet of 36: 36^6 = 2.18e+09 candidates, ~0.51 chance matches); container_hostname (1 value(s); 8 hidden of an alphabet of 16: 16^8 = 4.29e+09 candidates, ~1 chance matches); ssh_port (5 value(s); 5 hidden of an alphabet of 10: 10^5 = 1.00e+05 candidates, ~2.3e-05 chance matches). Open to a word-list search: runpod_volume_name (1), passphrase_shaped (1), runpod volume name stem (1), identity email, public in the commit headers (3), laptop hostname (1). Not recoverable by brute force: runpod_pod_id (2 value(s); 10 hidden of an alphabet of 36: 36^10 = 3.66e+15 candidates, ~8.5e+05 chance matches); claude_session_or_artifact_link (5 value(s); 24 hidden of an alphabet of 62: 62^24 = 1.04e+43 candidates, ~2.4e+33 chance matches); SSH signing-key fingerprint (1 value(s); 43 hidden of an alphabet of 64: 64^43 = 4.63e+77 candidates, ~1.1e+68 chance matches). Every one of these 22 values is in the history being published -- verbatim (21 of them, in blobs, messages or commit headers), or derivable from published material (SSH signing-key fingerprint: the fingerprint string occurs 0 times, but it is the SHA-256 of the public key embedded in every gpgsig header) -- so the form protects only this raw, not the release.

## (i) Secrets over the entire history

### (i-a) The block's primary scanners (not run by this script) and its fallback, run as written

**This script runs neither of the block's primary scanners.** Release v2 (2026-11-03): gitleaks is installed on this machine and runs in its own script (`scripts/audit_note_N12_gitleaks.py`, first raw `results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N12-gitleaks.md`, committed at e0fd85c), not in this one; trufflehog is installed on this machine and runs in its own script (`scripts/audit_note_N12_trufflehog.py`, the v2 raw `results/raw/2026-10-30_note-N12-trufflehog-release.md`), not in this one (`which`: gitleaks found, trufflehog found). The basis of that on this run: recorded by the v2 release raw `results/raw/2026-10-30_note-N12-history-release.md` alone (this run is a re-run). The fallback below and Code's blob-level scans in (i-b) are this script's own scans; gitleaks' findings and their triage are in its own raws.

The fallback, as written: `git log -p --all | grep -nEi '(api[_-]?key|secret|token|password|sk-[A-Za-z0-9]|rp_[A-Za-z0-9]|BEGIN (RSA|OPENSSH)|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16})'` with `/usr/bin/grep` (BSD grep; its exit status read from its own process, not after a pipe).

- `git log -p --all` output: 171,019,666 bytes, 3,723,384 lines (newline count; the stream ends with a newline: yes).
- grep exit status: 0 (0 = matches found). Binary-file notice in grep output: no.

Coverage limits of the fallback. (1) `git log -p` prints `Binary files ... differ` for binary blobs (the 18 .npz/.png blobs), so their contents never reach the grep; (i-b)'s binary-content scan below reads their string content (npz unicode members, printable-ASCII runs of every member, PNG text chunks). (2) HEAD's `.gitattributes` has 1 -diff/binary line(s) (`/data/m/ckpt/** -diff`); paths under `data/m/ckpt/` ever tracked: 5, so the limit now applies: `data/m/ckpt/G-CAL_qwen.json` (first added by `cf8eb5b9dd`, a commit after the base); `data/m/ckpt/Q-F0_campaign.json` (first added by `de6dfc54bb`, a commit after the base); `data/m/ckpt/Q-F0_id.json` (first added by `33cefe3f9e`, a commit after the base); `data/m/ckpt/Q-F0_qual.json` (first added by `f319f5380e`, a commit after the base); `data/m/ckpt/Q-F0_time.json` (first added by `dac74b7802`, a commit after the base). `git log -p` prints 5 `Binary files ... differ` line(s) for them and none of their content, so their content never reaches the grep; (i-b) reads them as blobs (`5e908334` 301,272 bytes, scanned as text: yes, lines matching the fallback's pattern: 1, `9890ab4f` 58,927 bytes, scanned as text: yes, lines matching the fallback's pattern: 1, `9b6db93c` 29,608 bytes, scanned as text: yes, lines matching the fallback's pattern: 0, `c32915cf` 1,686,719 bytes, scanned as text: yes, lines matching the fallback's pattern: 1, `f3eb2c11` 25,961 bytes, scanned as text: yes, lines matching the fallback's pattern: 1). The round-2 raw, a snapshot at `bab921ce52`, said this limit had never applied; `33cefe3f9e`, `cf8eb5b9dd`, `dac74b7802`, `de6dfc54bb`, `f319f5380e` changed that after it was written -- the case PI pre-flip step 1's re-run at the release commit exists for. (3) `git log -p` does not print commit headers (signatures); (i-b) scans every commit and tag header in its own section.

Locators in (i-a) are `<commit8> <path>:<side><line>`, mapped from the grep's line number in the `git log -p --all` stream: side `+` = an added line (line number in the new file), `-` = a removed line (old file), ` ` = a context line, `m` = a commit-message line, `h` = a header line, `@` = a diff hunk-header line (no line number).

### (i-b) Precise scan over every reachable blob and every commit/tag message

Input: `git rev-list --objects <refs> | git cat-file --batch` -- 1614 text blobs (every version of every text file ever committed) plus 409 commit/tag messages. The 18 binary blobs are not regex-scanned as text; their string content is scanned in the binary-content subsection below, and they are listed under (v)/(iv) and in the JSON (`binary_blobs`). Commit and tag headers are scanned in their own subsection below. Locator = the earliest commit introducing the blob, the path, the line within the blob. Patterns 1-17 are the builder's; 18-31 were added at the first values-check (the first checker's extended set); 32-33 at the second (base64 key-material markers, so a key body without its PEM armor is caught -- see the entropy note below).

| pattern | distinct values | occurrences (summed over blob versions + messages) | at HEAD | new since base | verdicts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| aws_access_key_id (AKIA/ASIA) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| private_key_pem (RSA/OPENSSH/EC/DSA/PGP/ENCRYPTED) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| anthropic_key sk-ant- | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| openai_key sk-/sk-proj- | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| huggingface_token hf_ | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| github_token ghp_/gho_/ghu_/ghs_/ghr_/github_pat_ | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| slack_token xox[abpr]- | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| runpod_api_key rpa_ | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| runpod_key_shape rp_ (20+) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| credential_env_assignment (RUNPOD_API_KEY, HF_TOKEN, ...) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| wandb_key (40 hex after wandb/api-key context) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| google_api_key AIza | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| generic_assignment | 2 | 9 | 2 | 8 | FALSE-POSITIVE 2 |
| bearer_header | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| netrc_shape | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| aws_credentials_file_shape | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| url_with_userinfo | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| jwt | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| stripe_key sk_/pk_/rk_ live/test | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| slack_webhook | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| discord_webhook | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| sendgrid_key SG. | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| telegram_bot_token | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| pypi_token | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| npm_token | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| google_oauth ya29. | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| upper32 (legacy RunPod-key shape: 32-40 uppercase+digit) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| putty_private_key | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| url_secret_query_param | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| quoted_secretish_assignment | 225 | 4515 | 3521 | 4463 | FALSE-POSITIVE 225 |
| pgp_signature_or_key_block | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| ssh_key_material_base64 (openssh-key-v1 / ssh-rsa / ssh-ed25519 / ecdsa / ssh-dss markers) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |
| pkcs_der_base64 (a run starting MII, 64+ characters: DER-encoded PKCS#1/#8 key or X.509 body) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | - |

Patterns (verbatim, Python `re`):

- aws_access_key_id (AKIA/ASIA): `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])(?:AKIA|ASIA)[0-9A-Z]{16}(?![A-Za-z0-9])`
- private_key_pem (RSA/OPENSSH/EC/DSA/PGP/ENCRYPTED): `-----BEGIN (?:(?:RSA|OPENSSH|EC|DSA|PGP|ENCRYPTED) )?PRIVATE KEY(?: BLOCK)?-----`
- anthropic_key sk-ant-: `sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}`
- openai_key sk-/sk-proj-: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_\-])sk-(?!ant-)(?:proj-|svcacct-|admin-)?[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}`
- huggingface_token hf_: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])hf_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}`
- github_token ghp_/gho_/ghu_/ghs_/ghr_/github_pat_: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])(?:(?:ghp|gho|ghu|ghs|ghr)_[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}|github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{22,})`
- slack_token xox[abpr]-: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])xox[abpr]-[A-Za-z0-9\-]{10,}`
- runpod_api_key rpa_: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])rpa_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}`
- runpod_key_shape rp_ (20+): `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])rp_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}`
- credential_env_assignment (RUNPOD_API_KEY, HF_TOKEN, ...): `(?i)\b(?:RUNPOD_API_KEY|HF_TOKEN|HUGGING_FACE_HUB_TOKEN|HUGGINGFACE_TOKEN|ANTHROPIC_API_KEY|OPENAI_API_KEY|WANDB_API_KEY|GITHUB_TOKEN|GH_TOKEN|AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY|AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID|AWS_SESSION_TOKEN)\b\s*[:=]\s*(?P<v>\S+)`
- wandb_key (40 hex after wandb/api-key context): `(?i)(?:wandb|api[_-]?key)[^\n]{0,40}?(?<![0-9a-f])(?P<v>[0-9a-f]{40})(?![0-9a-f])`
- google_api_key AIza: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_\-])AIza[0-9A-Za-z_\-]{35}`
- generic_assignment: `(?i)(api[_-]?key|secret|token|password|passwd|pwd|auth)\s*[:=]\s*['\"]?(?P<v>[A-Za-z0-9_\-/+=.]{16,})`
- bearer_header: `(?i)\bbearer\s+(?P<v>[A-Za-z0-9._\-+/=]{16,})`
- netrc_shape: `(?im)^[ \t]*machine[ \t]+\S+[ \t]+(?:login[ \t]+\S+[ \t]+)?password[ \t]+\S+`
- aws_credentials_file_shape: `(?im)^[ \t]*aws_(?:access_key_id|secret_access_key|session_token)[ \t]*=[ \t]*(?P<v>\S+)`
- url_with_userinfo: `\b[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.\-]{0,15}://(?P<v>[^\s/:@'\"<>]{1,64}:[^\s/@'\"<>]{1,128})@[^\s/'\"<>]+`
- jwt: `\beyJ[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{10,}\.eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{10,}`
- stripe_key sk_/pk_/rk_ live/test: `\b(?:sk|pk|rk)_(?:live|test)_[A-Za-z0-9]{10,}`
- slack_webhook: `hooks\.slack\.com/services/[A-Za-z0-9/_\-]+`
- discord_webhook: `discord(?:app)?\.com/api/webhooks/[0-9]+/[A-Za-z0-9_\-]+`
- sendgrid_key SG.: `\bSG\.[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{16,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{16,}`
- telegram_bot_token: `(?<!\d)\d{8,10}:[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{35}(?![A-Za-z0-9_\-])`
- pypi_token: `\bpypi-AgE[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}`
- npm_token: `\bnpm_[A-Za-z0-9]{36}\b`
- google_oauth ya29.: `\bya29\.[0-9A-Za-z_\-]{20,}`
- upper32 (legacy RunPod-key shape: 32-40 uppercase+digit): `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])(?=[A-Z0-9]*\d)(?=[A-Z0-9]*[A-Z])[A-Z0-9]{32,40}(?![A-Za-z0-9])`
- putty_private_key: `PuTTY-User-Key-File-\d`
- url_secret_query_param: `[?&](?:token|key|api_key|apikey|access_token|auth|sig|signature|password|secret)=(?P<v>[^&\s'\"<>]{8,})`
- quoted_secretish_assignment: `(?i)\b[\w\-]*(?:key|secret|token|passw(?:or)?d|passwd|pwd|credential|auth)[\w\-]*['\"]?\s*[:=]\s*['\"](?P<v>[^'\"\s]{12,})['\"]`
- pgp_signature_or_key_block: `-----BEGIN PGP (?:SIGNATURE|PUBLIC KEY BLOCK|PRIVATE KEY BLOCK)-----`
- ssh_key_material_base64 (openssh-key-v1 / ssh-rsa / ssh-ed25519 / ecdsa / ssh-dss markers): `(?<![A-Za-z0-9+/])(?=[A-Za-z0-9+/]{60})[A-Za-z0-9+/]*(?:b3BlbnNzaC1rZXktdjE|AAAAB3NzaC1yc2E|AAAAC3NzaC1lZDI1NTE5|AAAAE2VjZHNhLXNoYTItbmlzdHA|AAAAB3NzaC1kc3M)[A-Za-z0-9+/]*={0,2}`
- pkcs_der_base64 (a run starting MII, 64+ characters: DER-encoded PKCS#1/#8 key or X.509 body): `(?<![A-Za-z0-9+/])MII[A-Za-z0-9+/]{61,}={0,2}(?![A-Za-z0-9+/=])`

**High-entropy strings in an assignment or header context** (a maximal run `[A-Za-z0-9+/=_-]{32,}` whose left context is `<key>["']? *[:=] *["']?` (key `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,60}`) or an Authorization/Bearer/X-Api-Key/api-key/private-token header; Shannon entropy over the string's characters, bar > 4.0 bits/char):

- Candidates in context: base64-alphabet 25702, hex-only 26408. Over 4.0 bits/char: base64 23764, hex 0. Note: a hex-only string cannot exceed log2(16) = 4.0 bits/char, so the > 4.0 bar never fires on hex by construction; hex in a credential context is covered by the wandb/40-hex pattern and by the generic-assignment pattern above.
- Distinct values over 4.0: 664; verdicts: FALSE-POSITIVE 500, LOCAL-PATH 164. By reason:

| verdict | reason | distinct values |
|---|---|---|
| FALSE-POSITIVE | a file path (separator-delimited readable words) | 476 |
| LOCAL-PATH | a local temporary path ((/private)/tmp or (/private)/var/folders) in a recorded message; carries an OS-generated folder name or a session UUID; not a credential | 162 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | a snake/kebab-case identifier or label built of readable words | 18 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | an environment-variable assignment of a repository-relative lens file path inside a recorded command line | 1 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | a macOS per-user temporary-directory path (an OS-generated folder name) inside a self-test message; not a credential | 1 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | the name of a macOS software-update preference (CamelCase words), quoted in a values-check raw's evidence for an operations claim; a setting's name, not a credential | 1 |
| LOCAL-PATH | a shell variable assignment (RC=) of a Claude Code scratchpad directory under the local temporary directory (the project folder named after the laptop checkout path, a session UUID, a step-15 subfolder), quoted in a reviewer's evidence in a step-15 review raw; not a credential | 1 |
| LOCAL-PATH | a shell variable assignment (S=) of a cloud session's Claude Code scratchpad path under /tmp, cut off mid-UUID by an ellipsis, in a workflow agent's one-line summary field of a campaign-driver review raw; not a credential | 1 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | the file name of a local workflow script (a step-16 values-check pass label and a short run id), cited with a line number in a reviewer's evidence; a file name, not a credential | 1 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | the dated name of the design seat's step-17 block (a file at the repository root, untracked), in the verdicts raw's description of that file; a file name, not a credential | 1 |
| FALSE-POSITIVE | embedded base64 image data (notebook output / data URI) | 1 |

**Context-free high-entropy runs over every text blob and message (no assignment context needed)** ((a) maximal runs `[A-Za-z0-9+/_-]{24,}` (optional `=` padding) containing an uppercase letter, a lowercase letter and a digit, Shannon entropy > 4.3 bits/char; (b) maximal runs `[a-z0-9]{20,}` bounded by non-alphanumerics, with >= 3 digits and >= 3 letters, not hex-only, entropy > 3.5):

- Distinct values: 617; occurrences: 23747 (new since base: 23203); verdicts: FALSE-POSITIVE 198, LOCAL-PATH 414, IDENTIFIER 5.

**Round 9: the C2PA declaration.** The PI, 2026-10-10, verbatim: "declare it ahead of the N12 pre-flip re-scan as a known high-entropy false positive" (it: the C2PA content-credentials element each of the three filed 2026-10-10 renders carries; the 2026-10-09 renders they replace are not declared). Release v2 (2026-10-30): extended to the element each of the three filed 2026-10-22 renders carries, on the design seat's item C as put ("the C2PA declaration extended"; its answers, section 5, 3c, verbatim: "Extend the C2PA declaration to the three 2026-10-22 renders (one `<c2pa:manifest>` each, 7,684 characters)."), which the PI answered on 2026-10-28: "Yes to all"; the untracked .svg files at the root are not declared, by date prefix: 2 of 2026-10-06 (none carries one); 3 of 2026-10-09 (one element each). Code's implementation, not the PI's words: a value lying wholly inside the text content of a declared `<c2pa:manifest>` element is FALSE-POSITIVE with the reason: the PI, 2026-10-10: "declare it ahead of the N12 pre-flip re-scan as a known high-entropy false positive" (it: the C2PA content-credentials element of each filed 2026-10-10 render); Code's read-only decode, not the PI's words: a base64 JUMBF content-credentials manifest, no secret; extended to the element of each filed 2026-10-22 render by the design seat's item C ("the C2PA declaration extended"), which the PI answered on 2026-10-28: "Yes to all"; Code's read-only decode of those three, not the PI's or the seat's words: the same kind of manifest, no secret. Declared: 6 blobs (3 of 2026-10-10, 3 of the v2 extension), each bound to its repository root path (every path at which the blob occurs in history must be that path), the blob's length and its sha256 (`C2PA_MANIFESTS`); a value anywhere else keeps its ordinary triage, and explicit triage (TRIAGE16 / MANUAL_TRIAGE) wins inside a declared blob too. In this history: declared blob occurrences read 6, undeclared `<c2pa:manifest>` elements read 0 (their values triaged ordinarily); distinct values cleared by the declaration (every occurrence inside a declared blob, no explicit triage): none; distinct values inside a declared blob only that keep their explicit triage: none; distinct values that occur inside a declared blob but also elsewhere, so keep their ordinary triage: none; strict-base64 runs at or below the 4.3 bar that are declared blobs (marked in the sensitivity note below): 6.

The case that matters -- a private-key body pasted without its PEM armor -- is covered by the key-material marker patterns (above): pkcs_der_base64 (a run starting MII, 64+ characters: DER-encoded PKCS#1/#8 key or X.509 body): 0 distinct in blobs/messages, 0 in binary content, 0 in headers (-); ssh_key_material_base64 (openssh-key-v1 / ssh-rsa / ssh-ed25519 / ecdsa / ssh-dss markers): 0 distinct in blobs/messages, 0 in binary content, 20 in headers (SSH-SIGNATURE 20). The bar's blind spot is otherwise unmeasured: a structured secret that is neither a key blob nor assignment-shaped would need the block's primary scanners (pre-flip step 4).

Then run the record."; the session-URL fragments' entries in their parent value's class, `FRAGMENT_PARENTS_V2`, found 5 of 5: the PI's four, and v1's URL's fragment on his answer the same day to Code's question on it, verbatim: "Yes, like the four"); Code's own commit before the tag gave explicit entries to the values the v2 release raw adds (`TRIAGE16`, by source "Code, release v2 (2026-10-31), before the tag, read in context": 3 of its 3 entries found here; `SHAPE_TRIAGE16`: the backtick row of the v2 release raw and of any tag-run md); this raw is the history script's part of the re-runs at the tag commit: gitleaks' part is its re-run's raw there (`results/raw/YYYY-MM-DD_note-N12-gitleaks-tag.md`, `YYYY-MM-DD` that run's own date; by that form in `results/raw/` at this run: none), and trufflehog's is its raw of record (`results/raw/2026-10-30_note-N12-trufflehog-release.md`; trufflehog is not among the re-runs at the tag commit); the v2 release raw (`results/raw/2026-10-30_note-N12-history-release.md`) alone records the basis of (i); the fallback returns 25,565 hit lines (its credential-shaped rows: UNTRIAGED 0); 2 pass-phrase-shaped value(s) reported redacted (above).

### Identifier classes in content (every text blob of the history + commit/tag messages)

Counts: `occ` = occurrences summed over every blob version and message (a line kept across 40 versions of RESULTS.md counts 40 times); `src` = distinct blobs/messages -- in a class row, those holding any of the class's values (the union over its values, round 4: a source holding two of them counts once), in a value row, those holding that value; `HEAD` = occurrences in blobs present at HEAD; `new` = occurrences in sources new since the base. Context-discovered RunPod/container ids, the RunPod volume name (round 3), the Claude session link, the pass-phrase-shaped value and every SSH port whose triage is IDENTIFIER are re-counted exactly across the whole history (`exact` column: occurrences / at HEAD / new since base), word-bounded: no letter or digit may touch the value on either side, so a run inside a hex hash or a longer number is not counted. The class columns count context hits only (an SSH port counts only on a line with ssh/port/alias); the exact column counts the value wherever it occurs.

Patterns (verbatim):

- runpod_pod_id (context: pod): ``(?i)\bpods?(?:[ _-]?id)?\b[`'\"*]{0,2}[ \t]*[:=]?[ \t]*[`'\"*(\[]{0,4}[ \t]*(?P<v>[a-z0-9]{12,16})\b``
- runpod_pod_id (env assignment): `RUNPOD_POD_ID[ \t]*=[ \t]*['\"]?(?P<v>[A-Za-z0-9]{3,})`
- runpod_volume_id (context: volume): ``(?i)\b(?:network[ _-]?)?volume(?:[ _-]?id)?\b[`'\"*]{0,2}[ \t]*[:=]?[ \t]*[`'\"*(\[]{0,4}[ \t]*(?P<v>[a-z0-9]{8,12})\b``
- runpod_volume_name (a ..._volume name, or a word-joined name before a GB/TB size): ``(?<![A-Za-z0-9_\-])(?:(?P<v>[a-z][a-z0-9]*(?:[_-][a-z0-9]+){1,5}[_-]volume)(?![A-Za-z0-9_\-])|(?P<v2>[a-z][a-z0-9]*(?:[_-][a-z0-9]+){2,6})[`'\"*]{0,2}[ \t,]+[`'\"*]{0,2}\d+(?:\.\d+)?[ \t]*(?:GB|GiB|TB|TiB)\b)``
- runpod_endpoint_or_template_id (context): ``(?i)\b(?:endpoint|template)(?:[ _-]?id)?\b[`'\"*]{0,2}[ \t]*[:=]?[ \t]*[`'\"*(\[]{0,4}[ \t]*(?P<v>[a-z0-9]{8,20})\b``
- runpod_hostname (*.runpod.io / *.runpod.net): `(?i)(?P<v>\b[\w.\-]*runpod\.(?:io|net)\b)`
- runpod_ssh_gateway_user: `(?P<v>\b[a-z0-9]{8,20}-[0-9a-f]{6,12})@ssh\.runpod\.io`
- proxy_hostname: `(?i)(?P<v>\b[\w\-]+\.proxy\.[\w.\-]+\b)`
- dot_local_hostname: ANCHORED: each `.local` (not followed by a word char) expanded left over `[A-Za-z0-9.-]`
- container_hostname (12 hex after host:/root@): `(?i)(?:\bhost(?:name)?\b[ \t]*[:=][ \t]*|root@)(?P<v>[0-9a-f]{12})\b`
- ipv4_address: `(?<![\d.])(?P<v>(?:(?:25[0-5]|2[0-4]\d|1\d\d|[1-9]?\d)\.){3}(?:25[0-5]|2[0-4]\d|1\d\d|[1-9]?\d))(?!\.?\d)`
- ipv6_address (full form): `(?i)(?P<v>\b(?:[0-9a-f]{1,4}:){7}[0-9a-f]{1,4}\b)`
- ssh_port (number in an ssh/port/alias line): any 4-5 digit number (not a year) on a line matching `(?i)\bssh\b|\bport\b|\balias\b|\s-p\s*\d`, number pattern `(?<![\d.:\-\u2013@#+/])\b(?P<v>\d{4,5})\b(?![\d.%,])`; round 3: except a line whose every match is the word 'port' in `(?i)port\s+of\b` (the software sense: a port of code) -- in history 277 such line(s), carrying 22 4-5 digit number(s), which the exception keeps out of the class's counts
- ssh_key_path (~/.ssh/&lt;name&gt;): `(?:~|\$HOME|/Users/[^/\s]+|/root|/home/[^/\s]+)/\.ssh/(?P<v>[\w.\-]+)`
- ssh_key_name (id_*): `\b(?P<v>id_(?:rsa|ed25519|ecdsa|dsa)[\w.\-]*)\b`
- ssh_identity_flag (ssh -i &lt;file&gt;): `(?i)\bssh\b[^\n]*?\s-i\s+(?P<v>\S+)`
- ssh_fingerprint: `(?P<v>\bSHA256:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{43}=?|\b(?:[0-9a-f]{2}:){15}[0-9a-f]{2}\b)`
- ssh_public_key: `(?P<v>\b(?:ssh-(?:rsa|ed25519|dss)|ecdsa-sha2-nistp(?:256|384|521))\s+AAAA[A-Za-z0-9+/=]{20,})`
- cloud_account_id: `(?i)(?:\baccount(?:[ _-]?id)?\b[ \t]*[:=#]?[ \t]*(?P<v>\d{12})\b|(?P<v2>\buser_[A-Za-z0-9]{16,})|(?P<v3>\borg-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,})|(?P<v4>\barn:aws:[^\s\"']+))`
- claude_session_or_artifact_link: `(?P<v>\bclaude\.ai/(?:code/)?(?:session_[A-Za-z0-9]{8,}|artifact/[A-Za-z0-9\-]{8,}|chat/[A-Za-z0-9\-]{8,}))`
- local_temp_path ((/private)/tmp, (/private)/var/folders): ``(?<![\w.\-/~])(?P<v>(?:/private)?/(?:tmp|var/folders)/[^\s'\"`)\]]+)``
- claude_projects_path (~/.claude/projects/...): ``(?P<v>(?:~|\$HOME|/Users/[^/\s]+)/\.claude/projects/[^\s'\"`)\]]+)``
- passphrase_shaped (code word / pass phrase / password context): `(?i)\b(?:code[ -]?word|pass[ -]?phrase|passcode|password)\b[^\n]{0,40}?\b(?:is|was|:|=)[^A-Za-z0-9\n]{0,10}(?P<v>[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]*(?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+){1,4})`
- email_address: ANCHORED: each `@` with local part `[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]{1,64}` to its left and domain `[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}` to its right
- url_host: `(?i)\b(?:https?|ssh|wss?|ftp|sftp)://(?P<v>[A-Za-z0-9.\-]+)`

So (iv)'s clause, stated exactly: no model weights are tracked; and no residual arrays are tracked beyond the allowlisted `.npz` files EXCEPT the probe vectors in JSON named here, which README-NOTE.md names (its statement of this clause).

## Pre-release check: files not yet in history (round 2)

**Every audit raw must be re-scanned before the release commit.** The history scan above cannot see files that are not committed yet: the audit's own raws, scripts and README (which the end commit adds), the concurrent PROPOSAL-m session's pending outputs, and the PI's untracked documents. They are read here as plain files -- a snapshot at Tue Nov  3 18:29:31 UTC 2026, while other sessions are still writing them -- and scanned for (a) every value this run treats as sensitive (22 values: claude_session_or_artifact_link 5, ssh_port (number in an ssh/port/alias line) 5, identity email, public in the commit headers 3, runpod_pod_id (context: pod) 2, SSH signing-key fingerprint 1, container_hostname (12 hex after host:/root@) 1, laptop hostname 1, passphrase_shaped (code word / pass phrase / password context) 1, runpod volume name stem (the name without its _volume suffix) 1, runpod_volume_id (context: volume) 1, runpod_volume_name (a ..._volume name, or a word-joined name before a GB/TB size) 1), exact and word-bounded; (b) any NEW value of a RunPod-id / volume-name / container / IPv4 / SSH fingerprint / SSH public key / session-link shape, any new 4-5 digit number on an ssh/port/alias line, and (round 4) any email address other than the service address and the commit identities' own -- a possible third party's personal data, and the only such class this scan has; (c) the 33 precise secret patterns, each triaged on its match's own line (round 4). This part's own two outputs are excluded (the leak assertion covers them). A port value is a 5-digit number, so its word-bounded count can include an unrelated number that happens to be equal. A new 4-5 digit number on an ssh/port/alias line of a pending file stays untriaged (and redacted) until Code reads it in context; if the same number also occurs in this raw as an unrelated count, the leak assertion refuses to write until it is triaged -- a safe failure that a re-run under pre-flip step 1 can meet (round 3 met it: counts on pending lines where 'port' means a code port; the code-port rule under (ii) now skips such lines -- this snapshot: 1 pending line(s), carrying 0 4-5 digit number(s)).

8. **F8 (minor) -- the 'every scanned ref is an ancestor ... none could block a push' sentence says nothing about refs that exist only on GitHub.** APPLIED as text. Ancestry was re-verified for the 6 local refs (4 of 6 are ancestors of `origin/main`; not: `refs/heads/main` (`origin/main` is an ancestor of its commit; 1 commit(s) not in `origin/main`; a push of main would be a fast-forward of `origin/main` as this clone last saw it); `refs/tags/note-2026-10-v2` (`origin/main` is an ancestor of its commit; 1 commit(s) not in `origin/main`); see (v)). The sentence is now scoped to the locally visible refs. The PI pre-flip steps under (v) add a re-run at the release commit (step 1) and `git ls-remote origin` (step 2). On this tag-commit re-run, step 1 is this run; step 2 is not run by this script (it needs the network).
9. **F9 (minor, referent) -- the block names N12's output `results/raw/<stamp>_note-N12-scrub.md` (:84).** APPLIED. A referent note in the header names the three N12 raws.
10. **Missed: context-free secret scan (checker: 44 distinct, all false positives, among them 8 SSH-signature lines; lowercase+digit: 2 macOS temp-dir names).** APPLIED. A context-free scan runs over blobs and messages (617 distinct: FALSE-POSITIVE 198, LOCAL-PATH 414, IDENTIFIER 5; lowercase+digit runs 3; present at the base: 109 distinct, of them 2 lowercase+digit), headers (21 distinct, 21 of them SSH-signature lines) and binary content (0). The counts differ from the checker's by design: this scan has no word-segment pre-filter, so every run is counted and triaged, and paths are cleared by the triage rather than skipped. No value is a secret.
11. **Missed: extended patterns (JWT, Stripe, Slack/Discord webhooks, SendGrid, Telegram, PyPI/npm, ya29, legacy 32-char uppercase RunPod shape, PuTTY, secret-bearing URL query parameters, quoted key/secret/password/auth assignments: 40 distinct, all labels).** APPLIED. The 14 patterns (the PGP block among them) were added to (i-b) and run over every source class (round 2 added two more; see R2-6). Hits: jwt 0, stripe_key sk_/pk_/rk_ live/test 0, slack_webhook 0, discord_webhook 0, sendgrid_key SG. 0, telegram_bot_token 0, pypi_token 0, npm_token 0, google_oauth ya29. 0, upper32 (legacy RunPod-key shape: 32-40 uppercase+digit) 0, putty_private_key 0, url_secret_query_param 0, quoted_secretish_assignment 225, pgp_signature_or_key_block 0. Quoted assignments: 225 distinct, 4515 occurrences (40 distinct present at the base), verdicts FALSE-POSITIVE 225.
12. **Missed: content copies of jlens-qwen36 (checker: 37 files under jlens-qwen36/data/ byte-identical to tracked blobs, over 520 working-tree files).** APPLIED. This run read 579 files and hashed the 46 whose size matched. Matches: 42 -- 3 empty-blob matches and 39 non-empty, all under the clone's data/, 0 outside it. The checker's 37 / 520 was not reconciled at round 1 (its file list and selection rule were not in its report); round 2 settles it: the second checker's independent re-run reproduced 579 / 46 / 42 / 39 (files / hashed / matches / non-empty matches) exactly; this run: 579 / 46 / 42 / 39 (equal). Round 2 also adds the clone-index evidence under (iv), which is what supports the conclusion: no upstream jlens code, LICENSE, README or data is tracked.
13. **Missed: remote-only refs (`git ls-remote origin` before the flip).** APPLIED as the PI pre-flip step 2 (see F8).
14. **Missed: local unreachable objects (checker: 16 blobs and 1 commit).** APPLIED. This run: blob 1887, commit 9, tree 282 (119,485,404 bytes) of 5586 objects in the store. A push sends none of them; of the unreachable commits, 7 were on GitHub by the record (see (v)).
15. **Missed: 'continuity' over history (checker: 948 occurrences in 25 files; 'continuity password' 3 times, no value follows).** APPLIED. ## Values-check, round 2

The round-1 fixer's outputs (script sha256 `4f5859c5...723e`, md `4008afec...f5f4`, json `dc73e0c8...58f4`, all at `bab921ce52`) were checked by a second adversarial checker. It re-ran the script (identical apart from the stamp and the command line), found the redaction clean in all three files, re-derived the counts by other methods, and returned 7 findings (1 substantive, 6 minor) plus 8 missed items. Dispositions below; each carries this run's evidence. Actions: FIXED, DEFERRED (to another item), DISCLOSED.

1. **R2-1 (substantive) -- N13's pending raw quotes the full RunPod pod id `uimu...[len 14]#a39ed293` (N13 md:643, json:8321/8410/8916); this part's scope (history at bab921ce52) cannot see it.** DEFERRED to item N13 -- its files are N13's, and this part edits only its own. Kept here as a pre-release check, as routed: 'every audit raw must be re-scanned before the release commit'. Built: the new pre-release section reads every file not yet in history (untracked, non-ignored, or modified) and scans it for every sensitive value of this run, for new id-shaped values and for the precise patterns; pre-flip step 1 is now worded as a precondition of the end commit and the tag, not as advice (binding it is the PI's call). This run's snapshot for N13: `results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N13-provenance.md`: committed (tracked at HEAD, so the history scan above covers it; exact-recounted sensitive values in it at HEAD: 0); `results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N13-provenance.json`: committed (tracked at HEAD, so the history scan above covers it; exact-recounted sensitive values in it at HEAD: 0). The checker's six non-audit files with the volume or pod id: this run finds none (0 file(s) in the 'other' group with a sensitive value); they matter only if committed.
2. **R2-2 (minor) -- '1,793,055 lines' for `git log -p --all` is one too high.** FIXED. The count is now the newline count: 3,723,384 lines over 171,019,666 bytes (the stream ends with a newline: yes). Round 1 printed the length of `split(b'\n')`, which also counts the empty piece after the final newline. The grep's line numbers and the locator index are unaffected.
3. **R2-3 (minor) -- 'not installed (and were not installed), so the block's fallback applies' is imprecise: block :77 runs the fallback only if the scanners are not installable.** FIXED (wording and evidence). `which`: gitleaks found, trufflehog found. Release v2 (2026-11-03): gitleaks is installed on this machine and runs in its own script (`scripts/audit_note_N12_gitleaks.py`, first raw `results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N12-gitleaks.md`, committed at e0fd85c), not in this one; trufflehog is installed on this machine and runs in its own script (`scripts/audit_note_N12_trufflehog.py`, the v2 raw `results/raw/2026-10-30_note-N12-trufflehog-release.md`), not in this one; the Homebrew formula index is no longer cached, so the round-2 installability evidence is not re-read. The round-2 evidence (both installable, neither installed at the time) is in the round-2 raw; (i-a) states the scanners' state as this run sees it.
4. **R2-4 (minor) -- the jlens 'no code, LICENSE or README is tracked' claim rested on a working-tree comparison, which cannot see clone-tracked files whose working copies carry local patches.** FIXED. (iv) now reads the clone's index as a plain file (no git process in the clone): version 2, checksum verified, 62 entries, 59 non-empty; non-empty index blob shas reachable in the lab's history: 0; clone-tracked files differing on disk: 4 (`jlens_qwen/interventions.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `scripts/run_experiments.py`, `uv.lock`); of the 39 non-empty byte-identical files, clone-tracked: 0; the clone's tracked `data/` paths: `data/corpus/.gitkeep`, `data/lens/.gitkeep`, `data/lens/full_depth_fit.log`, `data/lens/full_depth_fit_v2.log`. All agree with the checker (62 entries; 0 reachable; none of the 39 clone-tracked; 2 .gitkeep and 2 lens fit logs under data/). Round-1 item 12 now records that the second checker reproduced 579 / 46 / 42 / 39, which settles the first checker's 37 / 520.

3. **R3-3 (minor) -- the SSH-SIGNATURE reason says the marker comes from the embedded PUBLIC key blob; it comes from the signature field's key-type string.** FIXED. The reason is now computed from each signature's byte layout (parse under (i-b) headers). This run: 20 SSH signatures, raw length 173 bytes x20; key-type string occurrences (byte, byte mod 3, field): [(14, 2, 'public-key'), (90, 0, 'signature')] x20; marker in armor body line(s) [2] x20; armor body line 1 (the one carrying the public key) distinct across the 20: 1; the marker line's distinct values: 20. So the 3-aligned key-type string is the one that opens the signature field (followed there by the signature bytes); the public key's own key-type string is not 3-aligned and does not encode to the marker. Checker: 173 bytes; offsets 14 (public key; 14 mod 3 = 2) and 90 (signature; aligned); armor line 2, 7 distinct; line 1 identical in all 7: AGREES. The reason now reads: 'base64 inside a commit's gpgsig header: the marker is the key-type string (string "ssh-ed25519") that opens the SSHSIG blob's signature field (byte 90, a multiple of 3, so it encodes to the marker), followed there by the signature bytes; the public-key field's own key-type string (byte 14) is not 3-aligned and does not encode to it; signature material, published with the commit by design; not a credential'. Round 2's disposition R2-6 is annotated with the correction. Verdict unchanged: signature material, not a credential.
4. **R3-4 (minor) -- the NOT CLEAN line puts a real redaction breach (N13's full pod id) on the same footing as the author's own email, which is public in the commit headers and outside the routing's redaction rule.** FIXED. The pre-release line is split: redaction-rule values (RunPod pod/volume ids, the volume name and its stem, SSH ports, the signing-key fingerprint, the container hostname, the Claude session link, the pass-phrase-shaped value), a new value needing triage or an uncleared precise hit -- NOT CLEAN, a blocker as drafted; and the commit identities' own metadata (the identity emails -- the author's and the Claude Cowork tool identity's -- and the laptop hostname, all public in the commit headers) -- listed for consistency with this raw's own redaction, not a routing violation, not a blocker as drafted (binding either is the PI's call). Pre-flip step 1, the summary and the JSON (`audit_files_not_clean`, `audit_files_author_identity_only`) follow the split. This run: redaction-rule group 0 audit file(s); author-identity-only 0. The author's address occurs in 771 author/committer/tagger header lines.
5. **R3-5 (minor) -- not byte-reproducible beyond the stamps: the order of tied categories in the pre-release sentence changes from run to run (a Counter's most_common() over values whose insertion order came from a hash-ordered set).** FIXED at the root and at the display. The exact recount now walks its classes in ID_PATTERNS order (exact_recount_classes()) instead of the set's per-process hash order, so every structure built from it -- the pre-release sensitive set, the JSON's `identifiers_exact_recount` -- has a fixed order; and the sentence sorts ties by label (most_common_sorted()). Code's check of this script version before this run: two scratch runs under PYTHONHASHSEED=0 and PYTHONHASHSEED=1, started together, give an md identical apart from the stamp lines and the command echo, and a JSON identical apart from its two stamps and the excluded-path list (which echoes --out). A run made at another moment -- this one included -- can also differ in the pre-release snapshot, because other sessions write the pending files concurrently; that is the snapshot, not the order.
6. **R3-6 (minor) -- the enumeration caveat is understated: besides 5-digit ports, the volume id and the container hostname are recoverable by enumeration from their redacted forms.** FIXED. The header's caveat is now computed per sensitive value (hidden characters of the class's own alphabet, candidates, expected chance matches against the 32-bit prefix), adds the word-built values (the volume name, the pass-phrase-shaped value, the identity emails and the laptop hostname: open to a word-list search whatever their length), and states that every one of these values is in the history being published, so the form protects only this raw, not the release. This run: volume id 36^6 = 2.18e+09 candidates, ~0.51 chance matches (1 value(s)); container hostname 16^8 = 4.29e+09 candidates, ~1 chance matches (1 value(s)); pod ids 36^10 = 3.66e+15 candidates, ~8.5e+05 chance matches (2 value(s)); SSH ports 10^5 = 1.00e+05 candidates, ~2.3e-05 chance matches (5 value(s)). Checker: volume id 2.18e9 candidates / ~0.5 chance matches, container hostname 4.29e9 / ~1, pod ids ~8.5e5 chance matches: AGREES (within its rounding).
7. **Code's addition -- pending files written by another session during this round put counts on lines the SSH-port rule reads.** TRIAGED, by a rule. A run of this round refused to write: the pending N16 decoding raw had gained table lines whose only port word is 'port' in "adapter port of e2a's rule" (a code port), with counts of rounds and seed streams on them; the pre-release scan held such a count as an untriaged port-line number and redacted it, and the same number occurs in this raw as an ordinary count, so the leak assertion stopped the write. A later run met two more counts on the same kind of line. Code read the lines, and every 'port of' in the history is the software sense too; so a line whose every ssh/port/alias match is the word 'port' in 'port of' is no longer read as a port line, in the history scan and in the pending scan alike (pattern note under (ii)). History: 277 such line(s) carrying 22 4-5 digit number(s), which the exception keeps out of the history counts. This snapshot's pending files: 1 such line(s) carrying 0 number(s), now skipped; new port-line numbers in pending files still needing triage: 0. Residual risk: a network port written 'port of <host>' on a line with no 'ssh', 'alias' or '-p' would be skipped too. The pre-release section states the leak assertion's fail-safe.
8. **Routing -- re-run at the current HEAD and name cf8eb5b (data/m/ckpt -diff paths now tracked) as evidence for pre-flip step 1.** DONE. This run's HEAD: `fee3706617` (later than `cf8eb5b9dd`: HEAD moved on); the commit that first added a `data/m/ckpt/` path: `33cefe3f9e`, `cf8eb5b9dd`, `dac74b7802`, `de6dfc54bb`, `f319f5380e`; named in (i-a)'s coverage limit (2) and in PI pre-flip step 1. Every locator stays pinned to the base `bbc9ffe72f` in the 'at base' columns; the HEAD columns are this run's HEAD.

Third checker's verdict, re-checked on this run: (i) this tag-commit re-run's part: clean (the v2 release raw alone records the basis of (i)); (v) PASS; (iv)-mechanical: jlens paths ever tracked 0, npz without an explicit line 0, weight-shaped paths 0. **The change.** (1) Explicit triage: `TRIAGE16` (value triage keyed by the first 16 hex of sha256(value); 56 entries) and `SHAPE_TRIAGE16` (the credential-shaped fallback rows, keyed by path, key and the value's 16-hex prefix; 74 entries), both in the script; `manual()` reads `TRIAGE16` before `MANUAL_TRIAGE`, and the fallback loop reads `SHAPE_TRIAGE16` before both. (2) The leak guard is verdict-based (as amended by the second check, below). Its candidates are every value printed in redacted form with the sensitive flag; every known sensitive value (the pre-release section's set: RunPod ids and volume name with its stem, SSH ports, the container hostname, the session link, the pass-phrase-shaped value, the identity emails other than the service address, the laptop hostname, the signing-key fingerprint; plus the session link's bare id); and every value of this run's tables, printed or not, that has a verdict other than FALSE-POSITIVE / PUBLIC / LOCAL-PATH (credential-shaped rows included, whose values round 4 never guarded), a mechanical record of a redaction-rule class, or a mechanical verdict on a value with a generated-looking segment. ### Round 5, second check: the checker's findings and their dispositions

The round-5 checker (adversarial, in memory, at `02b6a610`) found the guard safe -- none of the 14 sensitive values it resolved from history got through, in 7 contexts each -- and reported six minor findings, four of them latent gaps with no instance at its HEAD. Each is applied below, guard safety first; each control value is resolved from this run's tables or generated at run time, held in memory only, and injected on its own line into a copy of this file and, separately, of its JSON, as in (i)-(iii).

1. **The JSON was not reproducible byte for byte beyond its stamps** (`guard.controls.vi_round4_rule.by_exempt_verdict` took its key order from iterating a hash-ordered set). APPLIED: the dict is built with `most_common_sorted` (count descending, then label), as the guard's other counts are. This run's order: FALSE-POSITIVE 16, LOCAL-PATH 4, PUBLIC 1.
2. **The guard was described as stronger than it was**: the candidate rule took only values printed redacted with the sensitive flag, the known values and the four refuse verdicts, so a rule-class value with a mechanical exempt verdict printed in the non-sensitive form, or a METADATA value, was never considered. APPLIED to the code (the checker's widened rule), and the text above and the script's docstring now describe the code as it is. This run: candidates under the first round-5 rule 1133, now 1141; added by the widened rule 2 (METADATA | dot_local_hostname/METADATA-ECHO | dot_local_hostname 1, PUBLIC | email_address 1), of them printed verbatim by policy (SAFE_VERBATIM; the checker named two) 2, refused 0; added by finding 3's rule alone 6 (FALSE-POSITIVE | high_entropy 4, FALSE-POSITIVE | context_free/FALSE-POSITIVE | high_entropy 1, FALSE-POSITIVE | precise:quoted_secretish_assignment 1; mechanical verdicts printed in the non-sensitive form), refused 6. Controls: (vii-a) a generated pod-id-shaped value with a mechanical FALSE-POSITIVE verdict in the pod-id class, not printed redacted: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a redaction-rule class with no explicit triage); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (vii-b) a generated value with a METADATA verdict: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (verdict METADATA); the first round-5 guard refused it: no.
3. **A mechanical LOCAL-PATH verdict exempted a value with a generated-looking segment**: `triage_cf` gives LOCAL-PATH to a run inside a temp or projects path before its random-segment test, so a secret-like run inside such a path would have passed. APPLIED, the stricter of the checker's two options: a mechanical verdict -- FALSE-POSITIVE, PUBLIC or LOCAL-PATH alike -- no longer exempts a value with a generated-looking segment; only a hash-keyed entry does. OS temp-folder and tempfile names have that shape and are not excepted (the checker's other option would have kept them exempt); a temp path of words and UUID parts keeps its mechanical exemption. This run: values refused by this rule 356, of them found in this file or its JSON (plain substring) 0 -- they are printed only in redacted form, so the outputs still pass. Controls: (viii) a temp path holding a fresh 32-character generated segment, a mechanical LOCAL-PATH verdict, printed redacted: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a mechanical verdict on a value with a generated-looking segment, with no explicit triage); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (viii-b) a temp path of words only with the same verdict: exempt yes (generated-looking segment: no).
4. **Known values were matched only bounded by non-alphanumerics**, in the scan and in the 'carries a known value' check, so a known value glued to letters -- in the text or inside an exempt value -- was not refused. APPLIED as the checker proposed -- a known value of 8 or more characters is matched as a plain substring (case- and separator-insensitively, finding 5), in the scan and in the 'carries' check alike -- with two extensions: every other refused value of 8 or more characters is matched as a plain substring in the scan too; and the SSH ports (5 characters, below that floor) are now digit-bounded instead of letter-bounded, so the ssh flag form `-p` followed by the port is found, except inside a run of 8 or more hex characters, a digest, where a port's digits can occur by chance. This run: hits of the known values under the new matching, md 0 / JSON 0; digit-bounded port matches skipped inside a digest, md 0 / JSON 0; other refused values of 8 or more characters found as a substring: 0. Controls: (ix-a) a real pod id with generated letters on both sides: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a known sensitive value); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (ix-b) a temp path carrying a real pod id glued to letters, with a mechanical LOCAL-PATH verdict -- the path and the pod id both: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a known sensitive value; a mechanical verdict on a value with a generated-looking segment, with no explicit triage); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (ix-c) a real SSH port between two letters: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a known sensitive value); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (ix-d) the same port in the ssh flag form: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a known sensitive value); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (ix-e) the 'carries' check itself -- a temp path of words only (generated-looking segment: no) with the pass-phrase-shaped value glued to letters inside it, a mechanical LOCAL-PATH verdict -- the path and the value both: refused in the md copy yes, in the JSON copy yes (a known sensitive value; carries a known sensitive value or a session link); the first round-5 guard refused it: no; (ix-f) the declared exception -- the same port's digits inside a run of hex letters: refused no (as declared).
